We’ve been testing at-home laser hair removal devices for five years, bringing you the best ones with all of our firsthand experience and updating whenever we try new launches or underrated gems. To compile this full list, we:

Laser hair removal is a process where specific wavelengths of light are directed onto your skin in little pulses, targeting and heating up the hair follicle to destroy a portion of it with each treatment, says dermatologist Arash Akhavan, MD. Compared to in-office treatments (which, although pricey, are done by board-certified derms or trained professionals who know exactly what they’re doing), at-home devices have a much lower energy output—mostly because they often use IPL (intense pulsed light) rather than true laser technology—which makes them safer for you to use on your own, says dermatologist Julie Russak, MD.

Yes, at-home laser-hair removal devices do work, but they take consistency. Because at-home devices use lower energy levels than in-office treatments, they require continued use (think: weekly or every time you shave) to notice similar results, says cosmetic doctor Ana Mansouri, MD. Basically, they are not for the lazy.

No, laser hair removal isn’t necessarily permanent (even though that’s what they told us). Despite how they sound, these devices don’t permanently remove hair in a few sessions, leaving you hairless like a full-body wax. Instead, laser-removal devices only help to reduce the total number of hair follicles in your skin and potentially make your existing hair finer and thinner (only electrolysis hair removal can permanently remove all hair, FYI). Even in-office laser hair removal isn’t completely permanent. Typically, you’ll need to go in once every 8 to 12 weeks after you’ve done your round of treatments (which is usually between 6 to 12, depending on the area, your hair, etc.)

Neither IPL nor laser-hair removal is “better”—it really just comes down to preference. True laser hair removal only uses one wavelength of light (essentially, one precise laser beam) that targets just the hair follicle to help slow down hair growth. IPL (intense pulsed light), on the other hand, isn’t actually a laser. Instead, they emit broad-spectrum light in multiple wavelengths that spread across a larger area of skin, meaning they are less precise and won’t penetrate your skin as deeply. That’s why IPL devices are generally gentler and easier for people to use, which is why most at-home devices use IPL rather than laser beams.

That said, both IPL and laser-hair removal can slowly destroy your hair follicle to reduce hair growth over time with continued use. But expect laser-hair removal to be a bit stronger (and potentially more painful or irritating), which is why it often yields quicker and better results. Meanwhile, IPL devices are usually not as intense, so they’re great for using consistently at home or for use on sensitive skin, like on your bikini line or face.

Do dermatologists recommend at-home laser hair removal?

Yes, it’s okay to do laser-hair removal at home, according to Dr. Akhavan, but there are some things to consider. “At-home devices tend to be generally safe, though a real potential for adverse effects, like scarring, does exist,” he says. Which is why most dermatologists will suggest opting for the professional in-office route, if you have the means (laser hair removal can cost anywhere from $200 to $1,500 and usually requires at least six sessions spaced six weeks apart).

Just note: “If you’re using an at-home device, don’t use it on sensitive areas, like your face and neck,” says Dr. Akhavan. “And if you have sensitive skin, always patch-test first on a smaller area, then wait three to four weeks to make sure there are no complications.” And depending on the area you’re treating, you may also need to adjust your skincare routine to exclude skin-thinning ingredients (like chemical exfoliants or retinol serums) for three days before and after your laser-hair removal treatment.

Does at-home laser hair removal work on dark skin?

Currently, at-home laser-hair removal devices don’t truly work for dark skin. These lasers work by targeting the dark color of your hair follicle, and at-home devices don’t have the technology yet to properly distinguish between dark hair follicles and dark skin, meaning they can potentially burn you. While some devices might market themselves as being safe for dark skin, they are usually considering medium-to-tan skin versus actual deep-brown skin tones.

So far, there are no at-home devices that are safe to use on true type-six Fitzpatrick scale skin tones (reminder: This dermatological scale ranges from one to six, with six being “brown to dark-brown” skin). Most laser-hair removal devices are only approved for up to level-four skin tones, with four being “olive to medium” skin. But if you’re unsure where your skin tone lies, talk to your dermatologist before you try an at-home laser device.

What happens if you don’t shave before at home laser hair removal?

Yes, you should shave about 24 hours before using an at-home laser-hair removal device, otherwise the IPL/laser won’t be able to correctly target the melanin (pigment) in your hair follicle. This could potentially cause discomfort, like redness, swelling, and irritation. Because these devices work by homing in on dark, contrasting colors (think: a black dot on a white canvas), they need to be able to differentiate between your skin and your follicle to prevent errors or burns. FYI: Even stubble isn't okay for laser hair removal because it will create too much heat and can damage your skin (plus, it won’t even work to help reduce the hair anyway).

Just remember: Don’t wax before your laser hair removal. Waxing completely rips out the hair and its root, which will leave nothing for the light to target, and you won’t be able to use your device. Instead, make sure your skin is freshly shaved and free of lotions or creams.

At-home laser hair removal can be slightly painful for some, but it all depends on your pain tolerance. Many people describe it as a light sting—like a rubber band snapping against your skin—but some people don’t even notice any pain at all. Your device’s strength and intensity levels will also dictate how painful the treatment will be. In general, though, most people say it’s relatively comfortable when done at home (I would give it a 1 out of 10—it’s a piece of cake, IMO).

For the most part, yes, you can use at-home laser hair removal devices on your bikini line. But you should be very careful. This should only ever be done externally on your vulva. And keep in mind how sensitive the skin in your pubic region is. Generally, it's best to opt for the lightest setting, and go slow with the device. Of all of the at-home IPL devices we tested, our favorite for pubic hair was the NOOD Flasher Pro.

It's best to skip lasering until your skin has cleared if you're dealing with razor bumps. Reminder: Razor bumps are the tiny red bumps you get on your skin after shaving or waxing when the hair strand grows back into the skin instead of out. They're usually inflamed and irritated, which is why it's ideal to just let them heal and swipe on a soothing treatment for a few days before lasering.

If you’re just starting out with at-home laser hair removal, there can be a slight learning curve. Keep these in mind:

Like I said above, you have to shave beforehand to see any reduction in hair, and it’s usually recommended to shave right before. Usually, I’ll exfoliate in the shower the day before with a body scrub, then 24 hours later, I’ll shave and use my laser-hair removal device on clean, dry skin. Exfoliating the day before helps cut down on some irritation but still ensures a close shave.

While laser hair removal is typically well-tolerated, it can potentially make your skin a little inflamed and irritated. To offset this, always finish your treatments with a moisturizing body treatment, whether that’s a body lotion, body serum, or rich body butter. And if you’re prone to ingrown hairs, try incorporating an exfoliating treatment once or twice a week, like a chemical exfoliant pad or serum.

Remember: Laser hair removal takes time. One treatment won’t do much, and neither will a couple. It takes up to three months to start noticing a reduction in hair. Stay consistent (this usually means treating your skin once a week, but it depends on your skin type).

Only some devices are suitable for deep skin tones, and even fewer can work on light hair, so read the description of the product carefully, then scan the reviews for people who have a similar skin tone or hair color as you and see how they rate the device. We noted above if a device can be used on lighter hair, fyi.

You’ll also want to consider the size of the device you’re purchasing and the area that you intend to use it on, says Dr. Mansouri. Basically, a tiny device meant for your face probably isn’t going to be the best if you’re looking for a tool to quickly target your legs.

Do you want a device you can take on the go? Then you should definitely opt for something cordless (The only one we tested that we loved was by Tria Beauty, FYI). Or if you want something that’s going to be really easy to use, try the Braun Skin-I Expert, which connects to an app to help you track your treatments. Another loved feature by testers? A cooling effect to help bring down potential discomfort and pain, like the Ulike IPL device.

#6872. 7 Desert Boots That Can Survive the Winter

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Despite the name, these chukkas are built to be worn in all conditions.

Winter weather can get ugly. If it’s not wind and rain, it’s slushy sidewalks and unexpected snow flurries. You obviously need shoes that can handle a little precipitation, but the key phrase here is a little. You don’t want to stomp around in heavy winter boots, especially if we want to look nice. Dress shoes are a tough fit in winter, as are dress boots. Funny enough, between December and March, you know what our go-to footwear becomes? Desert boots. Technically chukka boots, but a few desert boots make the cut here.

But that’s something to note. The Esquire office does not use the term interchangeably—but the Internet does, so we’re going along with it. We know some of you—nerds, mostly—have stopped reading this article because you don’t think we know the difference between the two. We’re well aware of the square and rectangle-ism: All desert boots are chukkas, not all chukkas are desert boots.

If you’re not a nerd and want to learn something new, chukkas are ankle-high boots with open lacing and two or three pairs of eyelets, often made with leather uppers—leather or rubber soled. Desert boots are a subsection of this silhouette that typically features a suede upper (nubuck, sometimes) with lighter-colored, more flexible crepe soles. They are not as winterized—suede, especially, is known to take a beating from moisture—hence the name. The details vary here and there, but both styles can work for winter if you choose wisely.

We found seven pairs of chukka boots—yes, some of which are desert boots—with weather-friendly features, from water-resistant uppers to sturdy rubber soles. Most have both, so you can find a pair that looks sharp without leaving you entirely at the mercy of the winter weather forecast.

These chukka boots might as well be dress boots. They look sharp no matter what outfit you pair them with, and the ultra-refined, premium calfskin leather upper is naturally water-resistant; to a point, that is—we wouldn’t wear them in the pouring rain.

They’re very comfortable thanks to a soft, leather lining on the inside and open-cell foam insoles that provide supportive cushioning. And the flexible outsoles made from XL Extralight injection molding are a nice touch that give the boot some lightweight flexibility.

For a classic pair of chukka boots that come at a good price and have a wide range of colors and sizes, Thursday Boot Co. is the way to go. These chukkas come in a variety of colors and materials, but our favorite has to be the Mocha colorway in weather-safe suede. It’s rare to find a pair of suede shoes you can actually get wet without ruining, but these ones can brave the weather while still looking great. And with studded rubber outsoles, you have some extra traction and durability. If you want even more traction and durability in the soles, try one of the Rugged & Resilient styles with anti-slip wedge rubber outsoles.

Here’s another suede option you can actually wear during the winter; Sabah treats the suede upper of this boot to be water resistant, but you can still add a suede treatment for extra protection if you want to. These boots are handmade in Monte San Giusto, Italy, and you can trust that they were made with lots of skill and care. The insole and midsole are made from vegetable tanned leather, so they end up forming to your feet once you wear them, making them extra comfortable.

If you like the look of a gum-sole sneaker, you might like how it looks on a boot, too. With these desert boots, SeaVees blends the classic gum-sole look with the more rugged desert boot style, and the result is a versatile boot that can withstand pretty much any weather. With water-resistant rugged oiled leather uppers and natural rubber soles, these guys can get you through quite a lot.

These are going to be the least weather-ready on this list. You don’t want to take them out in a snowstorm, but damn do they look good. Imagine these worn to a holiday party, worn with a nice pair of chinos and fair-isle sweater.

The suede upper is a true not-water-resistant suede. But they do have durable rubber soles. The stitched moc-toe is a fun detail that makes these chukka boots stand out from the rest of the pack. Drake’s notes that these boots run small, so they recommend sizing up by a half-size when ordering online.

We’re starting to get pretty far away from the chukka heart, but you can see the chukka-shaped vamp and the ankle-y shape. Plus, when we think of rugged workwear boots, we think of Timberland. The upper is made from waterproof leather and has a TimberDry waterproof membrane for extra protection, and you can see that Gripstick rubber outsole gives you some distance from the ground and provides great traction as well.

And finally, we can’t talk about chukka boots without mentioning Clarks. The original Clarks desert boot was designed in 1949 and inspired by the rough suede and crepe-soled boots worn by off-duty British soldiers during WWII (as well as the shoes worn by polo players in India and South African veldskoen shoes). Today, the brand’s original style is still going strong, and you can get a pair for under $100.

#6871. Will There Be Fewer Gowns at the Met Gala Next Year?

Announced today, the theme of the 2027 exhibition explores fashion’s obsession with everyday garments like T-shirts, jeans, and trench coats

Just over two months ago, the Met Costume Institute canceled its John Galliano retrospective, the original theme for the 2027 exhibition, amid donor and patrons outcry—today, it has announced the new direction, and it’s as far from Galliano’s fantastical fashions as possible.

The 2027 Costume exhibition and corresponding gala is titled “extraOrdinary: Fashion and the Poetics of the Everyday.” It will explore how designers reimagine quotidien ideas for the high-fashion world and how wearing, styling, and caring for these garments shapes their significance over time.

The exhibition will be divided into two larger themes. The first, “Familiar Forms,” will center archetypal garments like jeans, T-shirts, biker jackets, and trench coats, looking at how designers reinvent these pieces and play with the symbolism surrounding them. The other, “Common Matter,” will focus on disposable materials like paper, aluminum, and plastic, and how they have been elevated to fashion.

“The ordinary is never quite as simple as it appears,” said Andrew Bolton, Curator in Charge of the Costume Institute. “A pair of jeans, a white shirt, or a sweatshirt can hold traces of a life—the habits, memories, and attachments that make something our own. Designers work with that familiarity, revealing unexpected possibilities within forms and materials we think we already know. The exhibition invites us to discover how much imagination the everyday can hold, and how looking again might change what we value and care for.”

Bolton began where he left off last year with “Costume Art,” and the reality of placing the body at the center of your design process. He was particularly interested in moments like Jun Takahashi’s fall 2024 collection for Undercover, titled “Watching a Working Woman,” which was inspired in part by Wim Wenders’s 2023 film Perfect Days. He created ensembles that joined and layered familiar garments in new ways to subvert and complicate traditional perceptions of identity and form. The exhibition will open with pieces from this collection and expand to include over 200 items, including Matthieu Blazy’s trompe l’oeil leather jeans for Bottega Veneta, pieces from Marc Jacobs’s 1990s Perry Ellis grunge collection, Brooke Shields’s Calvin Klein jeans, Junya Watanabe’s leather biker jackets, Charles James’s 1930s couture puffer coat, and more.

The original theme, which would have centered on Galliano’s life and work, was initially announced in late July. After donors and critics spoke out against the designer’s past antisemitic and anti-Asian remarks, it was then recalled in late August. This decision was unprecedented; the Met had never made such a late-stage switch-up.

Galliano was known for his objectively extraordinary work subverting themes of historical excess and grandeur. His shows were more like theater productions. Now, it seems they’ve chosen a balm for the raucous in skewing in the complete opposite direction, promoting real life over fantasy. That said, this new theme is purported to examine familiar ideas through the extraordinary lens of fashion. There is certainly nothing ordinary about a Junya Watanabe biker jacket.

There is certainly much to explore here. The theme reminds us how much inspiration comes from the people walking down the street every day, the uniforms that have shaped our perceptions of society. Those ideas are ripe material for fashion designers’ creative minds —and can cause quite the stir among audiences. Just think of how shocked the industry was after Marc Jacobs elevated the way youth culture were dressing, what we call “grunge,” to the runway with his 1993 collection for Perry Ellis. Or the fascination with those optical illusion leather jeans Matthieu Blazy put on his Bottega Veneta runways a few years ago. After all, fashion is only fantastical relative to what we deem commonplace. We need a bit of the the familiar to help us digest something radical.

#6870. Carolyn Murphy Steps Into La DoubleJ’s Kaleidoscopic World With a Joyful Collection

The “Sisterhood of Light” capsule turns classic La DoubleJ silhouettes into festive showstoppers.

There was a little kismet in Carolyn Murphy and J.J. Martin finding each other. When they met, Murphy had recently lost her home in the Palisades to the devastating California wildfires, not far from where Martin grew up. That encounter brought them together as friends and now as collaborators, as the pair teamed on a holiday capsule collection, “Sisterhood of Light.”

“She was the dose of joy that I needed,” the model tells ELLE of Martin. “We love a little woo-woo here, a little woo-woo there. But also what I love about J.J. is how grounded she is at the same time. And she’s obviously got great taste.”

While Murphy and Martin instantly hit it off, La DoubleJ’s maximalist, kaleidoscopic world is a departure from Murphy’s own more pared-back style. “I’m kind of a minimalist,” Murphy remembers telling Martin. “I don’t really wear pattern and print.” Martin laughed, and the two began exploring how to fuse their worlds.

The capsule interprets La DoubleJ’s signature pieces, most notably its Swing Dress, through Murphy’s lens. The A-line maxi, which celebrates its 10th anniversary this year and was the brand’s first dress silhouette, gets a luminous update in the Super Swing, a showstopper with embroidered dégradé sequins that shift from silver to gold. That luminous sensibility carries over to the Opera Swing, which features a gilded fil coupé jacquard, and the Mini Supreme Swing, a mod-inspired number embroidered with hibiscus blooms.

To bring the collection to life, Murphy modeled the designs at Villa Tarlarini, where photographer Kate Bellm captured the campaign against lush greenery and picturesque seascapes. Murphy looked to Marisa Berenson and Veruschka, whose influence can be seen in her wavy 1970s hair, for the shoot—an inspiration that spoke to her idea of world-building for the holidays and beyond.

“There’s [an] escape and fantasy with it,” she explains. “Even for the Carolyn dress, I was like, ‘Oh, maybe this is Robin Wright Penn in Princess Bride. Oh, maybe this will be a great wedding dress for somebody.’ And buying me a martini dress with this jacket is so cool. To me, that’s Hollywood.”

The capsule gave Murphy a chance not only to step outside her usual style, but also to find a creative outlet as she rebuilt her home and explored what it means to get dressed now. “I’m a fashionista of the early ’90s, and I love anything that’s classic with a twist,” she says. “I had these style icons back then. Carolyn Bessette—who I know has been super popular of late—and Narciso [Rodriguez] were actually friends of mine. They were my earliest definition of, ‘Oh, that’s what I want to look like, and that’s how I want to dress.’”

Now, the model is defining her own style. “After all these years of being dressed by people and being told what to wear, you get to a place in life where you’re like, ‘Well, I’m going to define my own style.’ I just think I’ve always been pretty consistent about that.”

#6868. The Apple Watch Deals You Need to Look for This Black Friday

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

We’re expecting record-low deals in the coming weeks—but for now, we’ll take $250 off.

It’s no secret that Apple has been dominating tech headlines this year. But between the news of its recent price hikes on MacBooks and iPads and the release of the iPhone Duo (the tech giant’s first foldable), not much fanfare has been made about the new generations of the Apple Watch. As far as we’re concerned, that’s a damn shame, because they’re well worth the upgrade—especially if you can find one on sale like we just did.

Apple Watches usually go on deepest discount during Amazon sales, Black Friday included. And while only two models are marked down right now (the Ultra 4 and Ultra 3), we know a thing or two about how Apple deals work. In the coming weeks, prices will drop and these wearables will sell out like hotcakes, which is why you’ll want to plan what you’re going to buy now.

Below, we’re breaking down the differences between each model—and peeling back the curtain on our price-tracking prowess—so you’re all set come Black Friday. If you don’t take the plunge on one before then, that is.

Commerce editor Luke Guillory argued that compared to other fitness trackers on the market, Apple was lagging behind with its lack of readiness score functionality—until now. The Apple Watch Series 12 marks the debut of the brand’s very own readiness score thanks to a revamped tracking system. Plus, the watch now measures your heart rate more frequently to deliver Heart Rate Variability (HRV) readings every five minutes. If you’re trying to pay closer attention to your health this year, it’s a smart buy, made all the sweeter when it inevitably goes back on sale for Black Friday.

For October Prime Day, this brand-new watch surprisingly dropped to just $350, so we’re expecting a similar—if not deeper—discount in the coming weeks.

While the Apple Watch Ultra 4 isn’t necessarily reinventing the wheel, it did get the same advanced health tracking and readiness score features from the Apple Watch Series 12, so if you’re looking to get your first Ultra model, it’s worth grabbing—especially when it’s $100 off. Not to mention, Guillory is partial to the new ocean-inspired, semi-translucent rubber watch bands.

That said, if you’re not someone who needs the shiny new gadget immediately or who won’t take advantage of these souped-up health features—you’re not going to miss out on much if you score an older model on sale, like this $250 discount on the Apple Watch Ultra 3. Associate Commerce Editor Bryn Gelbart went so far as to dub it the “best Apple Watch ever” when it was released last fall.

“I was never tempted to upgrade to the premium option, but the Apple Watch Ultra 3 changed my mind,” Gelbart said. “It turns out I didn't quite know what I was missing.” It has a bright 49mm display, satellite messaging features, a durable titanium case, and a long lasting battery (an impressive 42 hours under normal use and 72 hours on Low Power Mode). Gelbart recommends it for cyclists, runners, or the adventure-prone.

At 28% off, this is the best pre-Black Friday deal to shop as of publication, although this model has dipped as low as $600 before.

If you’re looking for Apple’s most affordable watch, we suggest turning your attention to the entry-level Apple Watch SE 3. It was actually the watch that converted Gelbart to Apple wearables in the first place, arguing that its beauty is in its simplicity.

“It's an accurate and predictive workout tracker. It counts my steps and activity and connects to my phone to play music and receive messages and calls,” he says. “If you’re new to the Apple Watch and want to dip your toes, this is where I recommend you start.” It has the same sleep tracking features, like nightly sleep score and sleep apnea notifications, but lacks the ECG app or hypertension notifications of its newer siblings. It also has an 18-hour battery life so you’ll need to charge most nights.

This dropped to a record-low $199 during October Prime Day, so we’re hopeful that a similar price cut will emerge for Black Friday, but this is by far the most affordable Apple Watch on the market, even at full price.

Our philosophy when it comes to shopping for Apple tech during Black Friday? If you find the watch with the features you need at a price you can stomach, it’s worth grabbing quickly. During Prime Day, a handful of watches sold out completely—and you won’t want your wearable of choice to meet the same fate before you check out.

#6865. A New Marni x Hoka Sneaker Is Almost Here—Here’s What to Know

The Italian fashion house once again brings its unique touch to the athletic brand.

Last year, when Marni and Hoka launched their first collaboration, the two labels came together to bring the world a collection of puffy, chunky, candy-colored takes on the athletic footwear brand’s Bondi sneaker. Naturally, it was a hit among the streetwear set, including the likes of Steph Curry. Of course, a lot can change in the span of a year and a half—and the latest sneaker trends are no exception. In the same way jeans have gone from baggy styles to straight-leg and even skinny jeans, fashion has taken its sneakers from their dad eras to silhouettes that lean a little sleeker. The latest result of these turning tides: The Hoka Clifton Marni.

“Shifting from the maximalist and oversized aesthetic towards a calmer, more essential form made sense with Meryll [Rogge]’s ascension to Marni,” Thomas Cykana, Hoka’s senior director of global collaborations, tells ELLE in an email. “This affords a simple but surprising design that cues premium embellishments, metallic hardware, and unexpected prints over an overtly in-your-face design. This shift enables consumers to both craft their uniform and express their individuality without sacrificing Hoka’s pure performance capabilities.”

Launching globally on November 13, the design is the culmination of form and function. Marni brings its unique design perspective to the technical shoe, not only with the chosen colors—Orange, Black, and White—but also with design-forward touches such as woven heel tabs, windowpane-patterned insoles, and three shoelace options for each colorway.

“Marni has a unique ability to take everyday design cues and make them feel artful and unexpected. That lust for curiosity and imaginative nature is what first drew our team to the label and continues to inspire us,” Cykana shares. “Our debut collection in 2025 showed how much our teams share in creativity and craftsmanship, and that trust led us to our unique spin on the iconic Clifton. The editorial tone they delivered to this project reflects the perfect sensation of everyday and extraordinary at once.”

The Hoka Clifton Marni sneaker will be available for $275 on November 13 at Hoka.com and select global retailers.

#6863. These 9 Brands Are Making the Best Dark-Wash Jeans on the Market

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Moody, rich, and sophisticated—everything you want to project with your dressing.

In the world of denim, think of dark wash jeans like a good Scotch. It exists within a broader umbrella, but it’s a little more weathered and more mature. In the grand scheme of jeans, you really can’t go wrong when it comes to different washes. For clarity, we use dark wash to describe lightly washed dark jeans, raw sanforized jeans, and the real nerdy loomstate shrink-to-fit stuff. Why? Because they are all, visually, dark wash. For us, it’s exactly how you should buy your jeans.

Jeans inherently skew casual. But the subdued color of dark wash allows it to pass for something a little more refined. Where other denim gets processed and faded after being dyed, dark wash hasn’t—letting it keep that subdued inky hue that veers slightly toward “polished trouser” territory. They’re more rugged than a pair of slacks, but just formal and sophisticated enough that you can probably wear them around the office as a sort of nonchalantly edgy work pant.

You won’t need to hunt far or hard for quality and stylish dark washes. Most, if not all, of the Esquire-editor-approved jeans and denim brands also offer darker colors—making it as easy as buying your favorite sweater in another color. Find your favorite below.

Rag & Bone knows what it’s good at and sticks to it. (It’s denim...they’re good at denim.) Our reviewers especially like how the brand approaches its sizing and fits. It’s a super simple and straightforward number system that starts at 1 and goes up to 4. Fit 1 offers a slimmer silhouette, while Fit 4 gives that classic straight look.

For their dark wash—which Rag & Bone calls “Solomon”—the brand uses its very own rb Selvedge, which it developed in partnership with one of Japan's premier denim mills. This will help the jeans naturally develop a nice patina over time to really show just how much you love your denim.

If you ask Esquire shopping editor Luke Guillory what jeans to buy, he’s going to tell you these. He’s already waxed poetic about Wrangler’s original Cowboy Cut in our denim buying guide, so we’ll just cut to the chase: they’re super flattering and at an ultra-competitive price (though he advises bigger guys should opt for the more relaxed cut).

If you’re looking for something under $100, Gap’s more than likely where it’s at. The retailer has spent a ton of time and effort in reupping its basics—jeans especially. The brand’s classic straight silhouette is, at this point in time, better than the offering from the big-name brand that starts with “L” and rhymes with WiFi.

People hunt for vintage Levi’s for a reason. These 505s are a reproduction of the brand’s iconic style for 1967. As Luke describes in our denim buying guide, the fit will “hug up top, drape down low.” “They're universally flattering and classic cut, so you don't have to worry about them going out of style,” he says. “As long as they're holding on, you'll look good.”

We’ve already rounded up a few Todd Snyder pieces we’d recommend buying, and these dark indigo worn wash jeans are one more thing to add to the list. Available in three different fits—slim, straight, and relaxed—it’s made from an all-cotton denim that balances comfort and durability.

This denim from Ralph Lauren’s Americana sublabel is an absolute classic. It’s got a distinctly Western feel with its deep 13.75 oz selvedge denim, while still offering that sort of rugged sophistication you want from a dark wash jean.

Little known fact, but Acne Studios actually started out with jeans. Over 20 years later, it’s still their bread and butter—with funky pieces that embody a “rock and roll edge,” as Luke describes them. This groovy wide-legged pair especially gives off a sort of “Lenny-Kravitz-meets-Steven-Tyler” air of vintage swagger.

We’re long-time fans of Buck Mason. As Esquire contributor Matthew Medendrop describes, the brand has been “quietly doing some of the most exciting things in contemporary menswear” for the last few years—and their denim is no exception. The brand’s “full saddle” jeans take inspiration from the classic vintage blue jeans, think Levi’s 501s circa like the ‘60s. Pair that with the deep dark wash, and you got one sexy pair of jeans.

3Sixteen is one of the premier brands for Esquire’s denim nerds. It gets into the nitty-gritty stuff we care about, but since it’s based here in North America—and not in Japan—it’s a lot easier to get your hands on. To break down the naming, RS means relaxed straight, what we think is the easiest to wear from the brand. The 100xk tag is the type of denim—custom woven for the brand by Kabuki Mills. It’s rough at first, be warned, but it softens and fades like no other.

#6862. All About Paige Bueckers and Azzi Fudd’s Friendship, Relationship, and Eventual Breakup

Bad news, sports fans: Paige Bueckers and Azzi Fudd have called it quits...and apparently, the breakup happened a while ago. But thankfully, it seems like the two are still on good terms. During a recent appearance on Azzi’s podcast, Paige confirmed their split happened “over a year” ago, noting that it happened before Azzi was drafted to the Dallas Wings. Ugh, my heart!

In case you somehow missed all the signs, the former UConn basketball teammates met as teens when they were both trying out for point guard on the USA Basketball U16 team, and over the years, their chemistry went from courtside to heart-eyes. And while they may no longer be together, at least we’ll always have their friendship, right??

Ahead, Paige Bueckers and Azzi Fudd’s full relationship timeline for your scrolling pleasure.

According to The UConn Blog, Paige and Azzi first met when they were teenagers trying out for a USA Basketball team at summer camp. At the time, Azzi described Paige as “this skinny little white girl” on the court. “I watched her play and I was like, ‘Oh, I have nothing to worry about this girl,’” Azzi said. “Like, ‘I have a better chance of making it than her.’”

After some friendly competition at USA Basketball, the pair formed a bond, and Paige reportedly started visiting Azzi in Washington, D.C. to work out together.

While Paige had already secured a spot on the UConn women’s basketball team, she supported Azzi and started a social campaign to get her on the team, too. Okay, P.B., we see you.

All of Paige’s work recruiting Azzi worked, because she committed to UConn in November 2020.

Shortly after, rumors that they lived together during the COVID-19 pandemic surfaced, thanks to a since-deleted TikTok of the pair bickering about getting sick.

Azzi and Paige are officially teammates! Paige shared a sweet sentiment about their dynamic on and off the court with The UConn Blog in 2021.

“I think we both hate losing but I think we both hate losing to each other more than anything. So we both want to win, we both want to make each other better,” Paige said. “I think that works on and off the court as well. I think we’re just here to make each other better and give each other opportunities and it’ll be fun to go at it in practice but once we get on the same court together and go against other teams, I think that’ll be good.”

Consider this Azzi and Paige’s social-media era. The pair is constantly posting content with each other across their Instagram and TikTok accounts, including that time they met Steph Curry:

That summer, Azzi shared a Paige appreciation post, too:

The pair pulled up to New York Fashion Week, sitting front row for the WhoDecidesWar fashion show.

They also posed for some adorable snaps during a UConn press day. We leave you with this cuteness...

Paige graced the cover of GQ Hype, and Azzi reflected on when they first met and how Paige supported her committing to UConn.

“This is so on brand for Paige, but she made a little highlight video of herself from her high school games, passing to her teammates and giving them wide-open shots,” Azzi said. “She presented it to my family and was like, ‘You see all these open shots I’m giving to my teammates? That’s going to be Azzi [if] she comes to UConn and plays with me.’ ”

During March Madness—and some of their final moments together as UConn Huskies—Paige and Azzi played a hilarious game of the whisper challenge, which was posted on their socials.

On April 14, Paige was selected as the first overall pick in the 2025 WNBA Draft by the Dallas Wings. Of course, her fellow UConn Husky Azzi was there to support her along the way:

Two days later, Paige spoke to Robin Roberts at Good Morning America about the draft and her uber-impressive basketball career so far. Azzi pulled up to the NYC-based studio with her, where they were photographed outside.

On June 18, Paige dropped 35 points and secured a win during a basketball game—which just so happens to be Azzi’s UConn jersey number—hours after wearing a tee with her face on it.

A few days later on June 21, Azzi sneakily soft-launched her relationship with Paige when she posted a mirror selfie with a phone case that read “Paige Bueckers’ Girlfriend.” She also wore her jersey during the Dallas Wings game that day, and they allegedly wore rings featuring each other’s birthstones. Cute!

A fan caught footage of the duo holding hands after Paige competed in a Dallas Wings vs. Phoenix Mercury game, while another asked the athlete for an autograph on a sign that read “Azzi Fudd’s girlfriend, sign here.” Spoiler alert: Paige fully dropped her signature, subtly confirming her relationship status.

Then, ahead of WNBA All-Star Weekend in Indianapolis, Paige correctly answered a series of questions about her “D1 Girlfriend” in a game with Wag Talk, including the name of the high school she went to, the year she was named Gatorade Player of the Year, her ranking in the class of 2021, and the basketball player she was named after.

Paige was also cheekily asked what Azzi’s phone case says, and when she was later asked to reveal the name of her “D1 Girlfriend,” she confirmed the romance rumors by simply saying, “Azzi Fudd.” Help, we can’t stop smiling!

Ofc, Azzi supported Paige as she helped guide Team Napheesa Collier to a 151–131 win over Team Caitlin Clark in Indianapolis...and according to this cute clip from ESPN, the pair looked fab as ever.

Finally, on July 17, Paige dropped this gem in an IG dump, clearing up any remaining doubt about her relationship status.

Paige and Azzi interviewed each other for Instagram’s Close Friends Only series, where they shared some cute stories about their budding relationship and longtime friendship. Naturally, Paige debunked rumors that she and Azzi share clothes as she jokingly put her former teammate on blast for stealing clothes from her closet.

“There’s never, like, a ‘Paige took Azzi’s clothes.’ It’s always, like...” Paige started, before Azzi chimed in to finish her sentence, saying, “‘Paige, pick out my outfit!’”

Azzi also referred to Paige as her fave WNBA player and asked what her perfect day off with her would entail.

“A mix of, like, chilling and watching a show, playing video games. But also, going to a nice restaurant and getting something good to eat, that’s different from what we eat on the regular ’cause of basketball,” Paige said, adding that there has to be some “kind of competition” through board games or otherwise.

Cute TikTok moment right this way (featuring a cameo from their former UConn teammate KK Arnold):

Just like her girlfriend, Azzi was selected as the first overall pick in the 2026 WNBA Draft, landing a spot on the Dallas Wings alongside Paige!

“I’m excited to play again with Paige,” Azzi told ESPN after the pick, adding, “She’s an incredible person, an incredible player, and it’s gonna be a lot of fun.”

The couple decided to share some rare comments about their relationship in the upcoming Apple TV docuseries, The Dynasty: UConn Huskies.

“Azzi ended up joining the team my sophomore year,” Paige said in the doc, which hits the streamer on August 21. “It kinda just meshed as soon as we met. She’s sweet, she’s genuine, she has a huge, caring heart.”

She later gushed, “We kind of felt a spark a little bit different than what would just be considered a normal friendship.”

As for Azzi, she shared, “Paige and I are a couple, and everyone close to us that was important knew, but it was kind of one of those things...it’s not a secret, but it’s private.”

She added, “We had conversations about, like, everything that was going on. When things weren’t going right, it was usually all Paige’s fault. She said to me, ‘I came back for my fifth year because I thought we were gonna have things figured out and it was gonna be fun.’”

At long last, Paige and Azzi revealed that they broke up...and that it happened “over a year” ago. Watch the devastating moment for yourself, below:

#6861. Nina Garcia’s Insider Guide to Istanbul

You’re reading Nina Garcia’s Insider Guide newsletter. This edition was originally published in October 2026. Sign up to receive the next edition here.

Istanbul is a city that trades in contrasts—East and West, history and modernity—and its layers go deep. Byzantine cisterns, Ottoman palaces, and a young, fashion-obsessed creative scene all share the same hills. You can spend the morning in a mosque from the 1500s, the afternoon haggling over antique jewelry in the Grand Bazaar, and the evening on a rooftop watching the Bosphorus turn gold.

It’s a city that feels almost unknowable. I returned in August, and I was lucky to see the city through the eyes of some people who know it best: Sinan Sökmen, author of Monday to Sunday Istanbul and founder of Istanbul Tour Studio, showed me around the city’s best-known spots and under-the-radar gems; and Sinan’s longtime friend, Ayşem Erginoğlu, guided me through some of my favorite shopping discoveries, from antiques to jewelry, in the iconic Grand Bazaar and beyond.

For this month’s Insider Guide, I’ve also drawn on the favorites of Turkish designer Dilara Findikoglu, who suggested a few more must-visit addresses, too. Throw in a few visits to the city’s famed hammams and you’ve got a city that blends history, shopping, and the best that two continents have to offer—an irresistible mix.

Istanbul works in every season, but spring and early fall are ideal, with mild weather perfect for wandering. Summer is glorious on the Bosphorus—especially if you’re stopping here on your way to glamorous, jet-set Bodrum—though it can be hot, and winter sees the city at its moodiest.

Getting there has never been easier, withTurkish Airlines connecting Istanbul with the entire world. It’s a fantastic airline: the seats are great, the space is ample. And the service is attentive, with an extensive and interesting menu that makes your in-flight meal part of the trip. My tip? Opt for a pass to the iGA–JETEX Premium Passenger Terminal, which includes fast-tracked immigration, personalized arrival and departure services, and a lounge so incredible you won’t want to leave. Even the longest journeys feel seamless.

Whenever you visit, you must schedule a private boat tour on the Bosphorus—ask your hotel or Sinan to arrange one. Seeing the waterfront palaces, wooden houses, and both shores from the water is the best way to understand how this layered, historic city fits together. Our boat ride was the highlight of the trip, and no matter the time of year, I’d do it again in a heartbeat.

Want to chat about my Insider Guide to Istanbul? Send me a note at ninagarciainsiderguide@hearst.com. I’ll see you next month. Until then, follow me at @ninagarcia on Instagram for real-time fashion and travel updates—and don’t forget to check out @elleusa too.

#6856. The 2027 Met Gala Theme Could Bring Jeans and T-Shirts to Fashion’s Biggest Night

“Extraordinary: Fashion and the Poetics of the Everyday” examines the clothes we wear on repeat.

Alas, the 2027 Met Gala has a new theme after the Costume Institute scrapped its planned John Galliano exhibition. Titled “Extraordinary: Fashion and the Poetics of the Everyday,” the show turns its attention to the clothes and materials we might otherwise overlook and what designers can make of them. The gala takes place May 3, with the exhibition opening to the public six days later. It’s a marked shift from the theatrical world of Galliano’s designs and could mean that we see jeans and (gasp!) T-shirts in droves during the biggest night in fashion.

Here, everything to know about the 2027 Met Gala theme, dress code, and more.

The dress code has yet to be announced. It will take its cue from the exhibition, as is tradition, but what that means for the red carpet remains to be seen. “I want visitors to leave looking differently at the clothes they arrived in,” Andrew Bolton, curator in charge of the Costume Institute, said in a statement of the exhibit. “With a greater sense of what is worth noticing, valuing, and caring for.”

Each year, the Met Gala’s dress code gives guests a way to interpret the Costume Institute’s spring exhibition. The 2027 prompt has yet to be announced, but the show’s focus on everyday clothing and materials—from jeans and T-shirts to paper, aluminum, and plastic—offers plenty of clues.

The exhibition will bring together over 200 pieces, roughly a third from the museum’s collection and the rest on loan or donated. Among them: Charles James’s ’30s couture puffer coat; pieces from Marc Jacobs’s grunge-era Perry Ellis collections; Brooke Shields’s Calvin Klein jeans, made famous by the brand’s 1980 ad; Junya Watanabe’s take on the leather biker jacket, filtered through the codes of Dior’s New Look; and Matthieu Blazy’s trompe l’oeil leather “jeans” for Bottega Veneta. The show will also include pieces that have been upcycled, recycled, and otherwise remade.

Despite the pomp and circumstance of the event, everyday dressing is already embedded in the Met Gala lexicon. At the 2026 gala, Bhavitha Mandava slipped into silk jeans and a three-quarter zip-up by Chanel; Kim Kardashian wore a head-to-toe Balenciaga T-shirt dress in 2021; and Doja Cat took the idea further in a wet-look Vetements tee dress in 2024. For 2027, expect the eventual dress code to invite that same kind of transformation: familiar pieces, reworked for the red carpet.

The 2027 gala’s hosts have not yet been announced.

#6853. Meet the ‘Black Doves’ Season 2 Cast and Characters

New assassins! New spies! New... [redacted], we guess??

The spy series Black Doves is back for a second season after two years, dropping all at once, on Guy Fawkes Day of all days, November 5. Need a refresher before you dive back into the Netflix series? Here’s everything you need to know about the new and returning cast of Black Doves season 2. Some of the new characters are such big secrets, we actually don’t have any details yet. Exciting!!

The first season, set at Christmas time, followed a politician’s wife with a double life as a spy in a covert organization called the Black Doves. When the man she was having an affair with is murdered, her handler calls in an assassin and old friend of hers for protection. The two investigate her lover’s death and discover that he was part of something bigger and more dangerous than they knew. Obviously the second season will up the already sky-high stakes, and introduce new threats. That also means new characters and cast members. So let’s get into it!

Helen is a spy who “happens” to be married to a prominent conservative politician.

Keira Knightley had a dual breakout in Hollywood in 2002 with Bend it Like Beckham and then, the next year, with Love Actually. Shortly thereafter she starred in Pirates of the Caribbean and Pride & Prejudice. The latter earned her her first Academy Award nominated. She was nominated again in 2014 for The Imitation Game and has two BAFTA nominations, for that and for Atonement. She was also nominated for an Olivier after starring in The Misanthrope on the West End. Other credits include King Arthur, Never Let Me Go, Domino, The Duchess, Anna Karenina, Colette, and Begin Again.

Ben Whishaw has played two of the most iconic characters in the British canon: the Q to Daniel Craig’s James Bond, and Paddington himself. He is an Emmy and Golden Globe winner for A Very British Scandal, a BAFTA winner for that and This Is Going To Hurt, and a SAG nominee for Women Talking. Other credits include The Hours, Perfume: The Story of a Murderer, Brideshead Revisited, Bright Star, Cloud Atlas, Lilting, The Lobster, Mary Poppins Returns, Fargo, Passages, and Peter Hujar’s Day.

Sarah Lancashire is an acclaimed UK actress who appeared on over 500 episodes of Coronation Street. She was on Happy Valley for three seasons, and became an executive producer on the third. You might also know her from Last Tango in Halifax, Julia, Where the Heart Is, and The Paradise. She is also a stage actress with credits on the West End including Blood Brothers, Little Shop of Horrors, Guys and Dolls, and Betty Blue Eyes.

Helen’s husband Webb was the Secretary of Defence in season 1 and is now Prime Minister.

Andrew Buchan played Felim Bichan in the first two seasons of Industry and Andrew Parker Bowles in seasons 3 and 4 of The Crown. You might also remember him as Latimer on the first three seasons of Broadchurch. His other credits include Carnival Row, This England, The Spanish Princess, and All The Money in the World.

Williams is a mercenary with a grudge against Sam.

Ella Lily Hyland starred alongside Aidan Turner in Fifteen-Love back in 2023. Her other credits include A Thousand Blows and Towards Zero. You can see her next in the ensemble series Tall Tales & Murder.

Eleanor is also a mercenary and Williams’ partner.

Gabrielle Creevy has been a series regular on several television shows, including In My Skin, The Pact, Three Women, The Guest, and Amadeus in addition to Black Doves. She will star in the upcoming supernatural series The Witch Farm and also had a supporting role in the 2021 film Operation Mincemeat.

Neve Campbell is a Canadian actress best known for playing Sidney Prescott in the Scream franchise. Before that, she broke through with leading roles in the series Party of Five and the film The Craft. Since then, Campbell had leading or recurring roles on shows like Medium, The Philanthropist, Grey’s Anatomy, Welcome to Sweden, Manhattan, House of Cards, and The Lincoln Lawyer. Campbell is also the voice of Simba and Nala’s daughter Kiara in The Lion King 2: Simba’s Pride.

Ambika Mod is a comedian and actor you might know from television shows like This Is Going to Hurt, One Day, The Stolen Girl, and I Hate Suzie. On the big screen, she was recently in Black Bag and Sacrifice. (And her Halloween costume this year was Bend it Like Beckham, which ofc starred Knightley.)

Babou Ceesay recently received critical acclaim for playing the antihero Morrow in Alien: Earth on FX. He played Pilgrim on Into the Badlands. He’s set to appear in the second season of A Knight of the Seven Kingdoms. His other television credits include Shardlake, Wolfe, and We Hunt Together. His film credits include Rogue One, Free Fire, and Resurrection.

Patrick works for an unknown, new organization, and offers to help Sam.

Sam Riley starred in the 2012 adaptation of Jack Kerouac’s novel On the Road. You might know him as Mr. Darcy, NBD, in Pride and Prejudice and Zombies. Riley played a raven in Maleficent and received critical acclaim for Control. His other credits include the 2020 Rebecca, Brighton Rock, and Free Fire.

There are three other new cast members this season. Sylvia Hoeks and Goran Kostic play Katia and Alexi Chernov. You’ve seen them before in Ride or Die and Jack Ryan, respectively. Plus, two-time Tony Award-nominated actor Samuel Barnett, who you may remember from Dirk Gently’s Holistic Detective Agency and who is currently starring in the musical Pride in London, plays the mysterious character “Jerry” this season. And finally, three more returning cast members in Black Doves season 2 are Kathryn Hunter as Sam’s old boss Lenny Lines, Agnes O’Casey as Helen’s assistant Dani, and Molly Chesworth as Helen’s nanny (and Reed’s daughter!!) Marie.

#6850. Netflix Finally Revealed the First Stranger Things Prequel Spin-off

Just when you think you’re out of the Upside Down, Netflix pulls you back in. On this “Stranger Things Day”—November 6, which marks the date when Will Byers first went missing back in season 1—Netflix announced the first live-action spin-off of its juggernaut hit Stranger Things.

On November 6, Netflix formally announced Other Worlds: A Stranger Things Adventure, with a teaser that confirmed the show will be a prequel set in 1950s Nevada. More specifically, April 19, 1955, in Marion, Nevada. (This also dates the show just before the stage play Stranger Things: The First Shadow, per Netflix’s press release.) The series is currently slated to premiere in 2028.

Netflix revealed little else about the series, except for its creative personnel. The Duffer Brothers are back as executive producers, along with Kate Trefy, who has worked with the Duffers on the original Stranger Things and co-wrote the stage play Stranger Things: The First Shadow. Director Joe Wright, whose feature credits include Pride & Prejudice (2005), Darkest Hour (2017), and Cyrano (2021), will direct the first three episodes. Production is scheduled to take place in New Mexico.

You can watch the teaser below, which shows a child’s bicycle, a portable radio on the fritz, and ominous dark skies over a restricted area. In other words: It’s Stranger Things, just a little bit older.

So, you thought Netflix was done with Stranger Things? You thought wrong! The hit show’s finale, which premiered on New Year’s Eve earlier this year, straight-up crashed the streamer due to a huge spike in user traffic. That’s despite fans and critics feeling split over the show’s resolution and fatigued by its years-long, drawn-out resolution.

This also isn’t new news. The Duffers have publicly discussed spin-offs before, including in an interview with Polygon in December 2025. The news today isn’t “It’s happening,” but confirmation of its title and period setting.

Will Other Worlds captivate audiences like its predecessor? Between this and It: Welcome to Derry, we’ve maybe had enough of spooky things scaring the bejeezus out of suburban kids on bikes. But if it ain’t broke, there’s clearly nothing to fix. We’ll see for sure when the show premieres in 2028.

#6849. A Tech-Obsessed Editor’s Two Cents on Early Black Friday Apple Watch Deals

The Ultra 4 and Ultra 3 hit record-low prices, so SE 3 and Series 12 discounts could be next.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

They say that good things come to those who wait—and when it comes to Apple Watch deals, I certainly hope that’s true. Ahead of Black Friday 2026, the brand-new Apple Watch Ultra 4 and the Ultra 3 are at their lowest prices ever. But when it comes to other popular models, it’s more of a slow burn; the Series 12 and SE 3 are still full price.

Shop our fave early Black Friday apple deals

So, should you shop a record-low deal now or wait until Black Friday? As a tech-obsessed editor who tracks these prices all year long, I’m sharing everything you need to know below.

The steepest Apple watch discount I’m seeing right now? $250 off the Apple Watch Ultra 3 (2025). This S10 chip-powered model offer health insights like heart rhythm reports, blood oxygen scoring, and sleep score tracking, as well as safety features like crash detection and emergency SOS. Plus, it boasts an impressive 42-hour battery life to boot.

Prefer a 2026 launch? The Apple Watch Ultra 4 is at its record-low price of $700. It’s the largest and most premium model in Apple’s lineup, with a 49mm case size and S11 chip. Most impressive of all, it has the longest battery life of any Apple Watch—50 hours, to be exact—which is more than double the life of the SE 3 or Series 12.

That said, Apple Watch Ultras are the most expensive models on the market, even with their current discounts. Personally, I’m holding out hope for deals on more affordable models like the Apple Watch SE 3 and Watch Series 12, which both dropped to record-low prices during October Prime Day.

The Apple watch deal I’m manifesting for Black Friday? The Apple Watch SE 3, which was on sale for under $200 during October Prime Day. It’s the most budget-friendly smartwatch in Apple’s lineup, but the S10 chip-powered device doesn’t skimp on premium features. There are tons of handy health insights to help you reach your fitness goals, from workout monitors that’ll count those 10,000 steps to sleep scores and even period cycle tracking.

This watch is available in 40mm or 44mm sizes, making it a great option if you prefer a sleeker look that won’t compete with your outfits. It also boasts up to 18 hours of battery life, with a fast-charging mode that’ll get you from 0 percent to 80 percent battery in just 45 minutes. Basically, this is your best option for a no-frills, entry-level smartwatch that won’t break the bank.

If you’re looking for more in-depth fitness insights and faster processing, the brand-new Apple Watch Series 12 is also worth keeping an eye on—it was on sale for $350 during October Prime Day, but we’re hoping the deal gets even steeper for Black Friday. This S11 chip-powered model boasts a longer battery life than the SE 3 at up to 24 hours, and a slightly larger screen size at 42mm or 46mm. It boasts the same safety features and fitness trackers as the SE 3, plus upgraded insights like a Blood Oxygen app and hypertension notifications.

My two cents? Even though the Apple Watch Ultra 4 and Ultra 3 are already on sale, I wouldn’t be surprised to even steeper discounts during Black Friday. What’s more, I predict that we’ll see Apple Watch SE 3 and Series 12 deals comparable to October Prime Day (perhaps even better) in the coming weeks, so it’s worth waiting if you want a more affordable model.

Shop our fave early Black Friday apple deals

#6847. Pierpaolo Piccioli Departs Balenciaga After 1 Year

The designer joined the house in 2025 after 25 years at Valentino.

Pierpaolo Piccioli is leaving Balenciaga after just one year at the helm. The news, announced today by parent company Kering, brings his brief tenure at the house to a close.

“Pierpaolo is an exceptional couturier and creative talent for whom we have the greatest respect,” Kering CEO Luca de Meo said in a statement. “Our ambition is to build on Balenciaga’s avant-garde spirit, honor its remarkable heritage, and continue to push creative boundaries, further establishing the house as a powerful and relevant voice.”

Piccioli joined Balenciaga in July 2025 after a transformative 25-year tenure at Valentino. The designer succeeded Demna, who oversaw a complete rejiggering of the brand’s house codes. Piccioli’s vision for the Spanish house focused less on the streetwear-inflected aesthetic that Demna championed, and more on a romantic, craft-minded approach. As he had at Valentino, the designer bridged the past and present to imagine something softer and more personal for the house.

His debut couture offering in July imbued archival references like the sack dress and the chou wrap with his signature approach to color. He also oversaw buzzy projects, including A New York Minute, a Celine Song-directed campaign starring Sarah Pidgeon, and collaborations with Euphoria creator Sam Levinson and Manolo Blahnik.

“We are deeply grateful for Pierpaolo’s contribution to Balenciaga,” Balenciaga CEO Gianfranco Gianangeli said. “Through his vision, he reaffirmed the couture origins of the house, bringing his considered approach to craftsmanship, construction, and creativity. I would like to sincerely thank him for his dedication, generosity, and collaboration.”

Piccioli’s final Balenciaga collection, which mixed couture techniques with everyday silhouettes, took its bow at Paris Fashion Week in early October. What’s next for the designer is still a question mark, and Kering has yet to name his successor.

#6846. The 16 Best New Hotels in New York City

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

In the last five years, the Big Apple has seen some of its biggest openings and major renovations in history. These are the best of the bunch.

Visiting New York City makes for a jam-packed trip. From shows on Broadway to walks through Central Park, from exhibits at the museums (Met? MoMA? Take your pick!) to shopping Fifth Avenue, days here are booked from start to finish. Of course, it’s then that the real fun starts—Manhattan is full of the best bars and restaurants in the world.

Given the length of the to-do list, some travelers think that where they stay while in town doesn’t matter. We disagree. Just because you won’t spend the bulk of your time lounging about in your hotel robe doesn’t mean you don’t need a plush option to coddle you after an afternoon spent zipping from sight to sight or a long night out on the tiles. A big bed, crisp linens, and a killer lobby bar can make a visit, after all.

New York City is a town obsessed with the new. (It’s even right there in the name: New York City.) And over the last five years it has seen an influx of hotels that are as much the destination as the line items on any itinerary. From the namesake brands that finally have a place to call home here, like the Faena, to the most iconic renovation of the decade at the Waldorf Astoria, here is exactly where you should be booking right now.

Faena shelters you from the city’s chaos, but it’s New York through and through. To the west, views of the Hudson River Greenway and Little Island. To the east, you can high-five people on the High Line from the terrace of the Living Room bar. The most N.Y.C. touch? The staff’s suits are nicer than yours. Rooms from $1,395. —Dave Holmes

On the hotel’s website, it says that “to tell the story of New York is, in some sense, to tell the story of the Waldorf Astoria.” For once, the marketing is correct. Like Manhattan, the hotel’s story is a tale of outsize real estate ambitions. A global standard for hospitality ever since the combination of the Waldorf Hotel and the Astoria Hotel in 1897, the property became the prize in the most expensive hotel sale ever in 2014. Three years later, the new owners closed it for renovations. The project cost $2 billion and took eight years, during which time the property was traded again. The wait was worth it. From the rooms to the restaurants (there are three) to the sprawling lobby, where Cole Porter’s piano anchors the room, to the Guerlain Wellness Spa, the hotel outright dazzles. It’s the talk of Manhattan. And word is it’s already back up for sale. Rooms from $1,295. —Madison Vain

An understated facade on Thompson Street hides the sexiest hotel in SoHo. The Manner is the first of the latest brand by Standard International, and its flagship location is codesigned by the Standard’s Verena Haller and Milanese architect Hannes Peer, so the whole place is Studio 54 stylish, yet comfortable. Speakeasy Sloane’s is a great place for a cocktail and—we swear—the best chicken nuggets in town, while the Apartment is a cozy common space to use as your living room. Check out the penthouse, modeled after Halston’s office, and dream of the party you’ll throw there. There’s no better center of operations for your next New York trip. Rooms from $799. —D.H.

Don’t let yourself get swept away by the history. An Art Deco gem built in the 1920s at 76th and Madison, the Surrey was a longtime landmark in New York’s glitziest neighborhood, playing host to the likes of JFK and Bette Davis. It closed after a century and, following a multiyear renovation, recently reopened as the first North American property under global luxury hotel group Corinthia. From the outside, the property could easily pass for a residential building. Inside, you’ll find 70 rooms and 30 suites, each flawless. Muted terra-cotta-colored walls, crisp linens, and walk-in marble showers make for plush surroundings. The spa is small but divine. And the lobby bar and restaurant, Casa Tua (of Aspen, Miami, and Paris notoriety), is one of the hardest-to-get tables in town. Movie stars, politicians, famous divorcées, influencers—everyone here is someone. Including you. Rooms from $1,000. —M.V.

The Fifth Avenue Hotel leans into what New Yorkers with good taste who live in small spaces know is the secret to making their apartments feel luxurious: Love every single piece of furniture, adore every square inch. Nothing is wasted. If you’re lucky, you’ll get something like the rooms and hallways of the Fifth Avenue Hotel. It’s like rich-aunt maximalism: Missoni-style upholstered chairs and couches, an armoire that’s actually a giant bar adorned with dragons and birds. The wallpaper looks as though it’s hand-painted. Lots of tapestries and tufted pink walls. Unlike in many N.Y.C. apartments, you’ll get a great night’s sleep here—the room feels pin-drop quiet, and the bathrooms are serene marble white. This is New York, but you might not leave the hotel. There’s in-room martini service, and downstairs is one of the city’s best new restaurants, the old-school-luxe Café Carmellini, as well as the Portrait Bar, home to some of the town’s best martinis (outside of your room). Rooms from $895 —K.S.

Hotels where you pay by the month can be dicier than a Vegas craps table. But Maison Hudson is the polar opposite. Located on the banks of the Hudson and at the fringe of the West Village, the property straddles a blurry line between ultra-luxurious hotel and supremely plush apartment. The minimum stay is a month, and the price starts at thirty g’s. Sounds absurdly high? Maybe. But when suites in N.Y.C. go for several thousand dollars per night, a stay at Maison Hudson can feel kind of like a good deal over time. Boy math! Okay, so say you hit it big on a stock investment/crypto/inheritance and want to live like a 0.01 percenter in New York’s coolest neighborhood. Here’s what you get: a chic, tastefully decorated, ridiculously massive thousand-square-foot living space, complete with heated bathroom floors, automatic shades, a fully stocked kitchen, and an in-unit washer/dryer. The concierge—who is really like your own personal assistant—will get you anything you could possibly desire. (Think concert tickets, a rare bottle of wine, an endangered animal to eat for dinner—kidding!) There’s an in-house spa that offers massages, facials, and antiaging treatments in a Zen-like setting. Marius, the French-inspired restaurant helmed by Michelin-star chef Sébastien Sanjou, feels intimate with only a handful of tables. While the eatery is open to the public, don’t worry: Maison Hudson guests get first priority for seatings, natch. Rooms from $32,500 per month —Daniel Dumas

The brand’s three Manhattan locations have long been the smart traveler’s pick for affordable city lodging, and now, like so many city people, the Arlo group has made the jump into Brooklyn. On the site of the former Hotel Williamsburg, the most eye-catching of the luxury hotels to hit the hip ’hood in the 2010s, Arlo Williamsburg is the exact right home base for a New York City getaway. Brooklyn buzzes outside each room’s floor-to-ceiling windows, while a rooftop pool and a bar and event space inside the Arlo’s landmark water tower offer views of the full sweep of Manhattan’s skyline. It seems so close you could touch it, but with all of Billyburg’s bars, restaurants, shops, and general people-watching just steps from the front door, you may decide to skip the quick trip on the L and stay local. The company’s focus on art and interesting programming has survived the trip across the river. And out here you can stand in the middle of your room, extend both arms, and not touch a wall. Try that in Manhattan. Rooms from $400 —D.H.

On one end of the travel-fantasy spectrum, there is the blissed-out, blue-skies-and-beaches vacation. The stuff postcards are made of. Nothing wrong with seeking that. Toward the other end is the kind where you imagine yourself as a neighborhood local. You tap into the rhythm of the vibrant daily life in the blocks around you and feel as if you’ve merged with a hipper, alternate-universe version of yourself. That’s what Nine Orchard will do for you.

We know the “hotels for locals” shtick, where there’s a lobby filled with folks on laptops. This is not that. Nine Orchard is more about creating the fantasy of residence. It starts with your room number. 4L, 3G, 8A. You might think you’ve stepped into someone’s apartment. Each room has an appreciation for the analog—the handmade ceramic lamps, the paintings from local artists, the wooden Ojas speakers that tune in to stations curated by DJ Stretch Armstrong and Devon Turnbull. You’ll want to hole up and write a memoir here.

Lying in a cloudlike king bed, facing downtown, with One World Trade Center glimmering in the distance, I watched the sunset, thirty-four stories in the sky, and thought, What a thing to experience in a city where you thought you’d seen it all. As a gleaming, fifty-story glass tower, the Ritz-Carlton New York NoMad offers that Ritz-Carlton tranquility, but the bars and restaurants here have made it an unlikely nightlife destination in a decidedly non-touristy hood. You’ll have a sanctuary in your room with a tub—rare in N.Y.C.—but there’s also a buzzy lobby bar, the José Andrés rooftop lounge Nubeluz as well as his Bazaar Meat on the second floor, the first-floor Mediterranean delight Zaytinya, and, in the summer, the vibrant fifth-floor terrace bar, Dahlia’s Garden. It’s an uncommon New York hotel combo, where you can be serene or all-out energetic. Rooms from approx. $1,500 —K.S.

You can’t talk about the Hotel Chelsea without checking off the names of its famous tenants and guests. The building, finished in 1884, was conceived as a kind of commune for musicians and novelists and has had a bohemian artistic energy since then. Mark Twain, Stanley Kubrick, Patti Smith, Andy Warhol, and Jimi Hendrix are just some of the notable folks who have graced this Queen Anne–style building over the past century. Today it might lack a raucous clientele, but that’s all for a better night’s sleep. Expect laid-back service, marble-and-brass bathrooms, animal-print furniture. From the pitch-perfect El Quijote, a Spanish restaurant that opened in 1930, to the iconic stained-glass windows and wrought-iron balconies, checking into the Chelsea feels like entering a time warp. Rooms from approx. $300 —K.S.

Want to see something beautiful? Come stand on the corner of Wall Street and Water in Lower Manhattan and stare at this gorgeous mass of concrete. The Paspaley family, Australians who were pioneers in the pearl industry, lovingly and painstakingly brought the beaux arts facade of the Wall Street Hotel to life over the course of several years. And what’s inside is even better. Spacious suites (for Manhattan!), oversize bathrooms (see previous parenthetical), a chic-meets-fun bar that draws locals and guests, plus an absolutely killer in-house French brasserie—this place doesn’t miss. Rooms from $465 —M.V.

You’ll quickly lose track of how many food and beverage options there are at the iconic London brand’s NoMad location. Rooftop bar, lobby bar, library (with bar service), a trendy Italian spot, a private table in a water tower, and a formal dining room for table-side service. All have their own vibe and are worthy of inspection, but only some are available to hotel guests. The rest are reserved for Ned members. But worry not; there’s plenty of fun to be had for those who book here. Rooms from $375 —M.V.

The committed traveler, it stands to reason, always looks for authentic local color. So a stay in New York’s TriBeCa neighborhood should naturally involve a few nights in a soaring, modernist hymn to the twenty-four-hour possibilities of twenty-first-century New York. Right? Non. Hotel Barrière Fouquet’s, which opened recently on Greenwich Street, whiffs unmistakably and quite charmingly of Paris. For more than a hundred years, Fouquet’s existed as one of the grandest of all Parisian café-restaurants. In 2006, it entered the hotel business when it was reopened with more than one hundred luxury rooms added by the Barrière Group above and around the original café. The New York hotel is the first U.S. venture of the group, which maintains properties in some of the most chichi resorts in Europe. Its inherent Frenchness is both obvious and lightly done. The staff offer a singsongy “Bonjour” when you pass them in the corridor. In the rooms, the furnishings are postmodern, curvy takes on deco, and the toile de Jouy–style wallpaper is about as French as you can get in room decor, until you stand up close and realize it’s actually composed entirely of cartoony New York street scenes. With a relatively low room count—just ninety-seven in total, including a two-story Grand Appartement Terrasse—the hotel feels quiet and intimate. There’s just a single low-key bar, the Brasserie Fouquet’s New York, and the Par Ici Café, which doubles as the breakfast spot and where, reassuringly, the prices have been imported from Paris along with the style. Rooms from $900 —Nick Sullivan

As with many Aces around the globe, the Brooklyn outpost is very much about building a cool community hangout the only way the Ace can. The purpose-built, brutalist structure of concrete and wood is home to a lobby with a buzzing bar anchoring all of the loungy furniture, where folks Zoom on laptops by day and old-fashioneds are being stirred come nightfall. Each month features different DJs spinning soul, and the Atrium on the second floor often hosts poetry readings and film screenings. The woodshop-chic rooms aren’t bad either. Rooms from $199 —Omar Mamoon

Welcome to la dolce vita, a place where Loro Piana cashmere lines the walls of your presidential suite, where custom scents fill the air, where fireplaces roar and Bellinis are handed out by waiters in white jackets. The first of several membership clubs/boutique hotels planned by Italian hospitality’s best-known ambassadors, Casa Cipriani—located on South Street in Lower Manhattan—has had glitzy New Yorkers jockeying for access ever since it opened in 2022. The pitch was a gamble (there’s a dress code, and prices are astronomical), but it turns out that a lot of luxury feels just right these days. Rooms from approx. $750 —M.V.

Beyond the modern extension of Penn Station, you’ll come across an undulating tower seemingly conjured out of nowhere in an area that was, just a few years ago, a kind of no-man’s-land. For the trek across Ninth Avenue, the Pendry epitomizes Cali-luxe: plush, earth-toned rooms where curved windows create nooks to dwell in and admire the new urban vista. Downstairs, it’s time for a mini bar crawl. Bar Pendry is a gold-leaf-adorned jewel box for a more clandestine drink, and Chez Zou is like a belle epoque garden complete with deft cocktails and a secret cheeseburger. Rooms from $725 —K.S.

#6836. Seiko’s Affordable Hideo Kojima Watches Are a Gamer's Dream—but There's a Catch

The Japanese brand has teamed up with Kojima Productions to celebrate its 10th anniversary with two limited-edition timepieces.

If you're a fan of Hideo Kojima's video games, you might want to take a look at Seiko's later collaboration. The watchmaker has teamed up with Kojima Productions, the studio behind Death Stranding, to release two limited-edition watches celebrating its 10th anniversary, both exclusive to Japan.

And, in keeping with Kojima's rather distinctive creative universe, these aren't your typical Seikos.

The new Ludens watches come in two colourways, black and white, with just 300 of each available. Both take inspiration from Ludens, the futuristic, spacesuit-wearing mascot of Kojima Productions, whose illustration takes pride and place on the dial alongside the studio's motto From Sapiens to Ludens.

The white edition features a silver-and-white dial paired with a patterned white strap, while the black version offers a darker alternative. Turn either watch over and you'll find an engraved illustration of Ludens on the case back, alongside the Seiko logo and an individual serial number.

As for what's inside, both watches are powered by Seiko's battery-operated 6N01 quartz movement, which offers an accuracy of approximately 15 seconds per month and a battery life of around three years. There's also a stainless steel case, Hardlex crystal, and everyday water resistance.

The black edition is priced at ¥55,000 (approximately $350), while the white version costs ¥57,600 (around $365). The latter also comes with a ticket to Kojima Productions' Beyond the Strand 10th-anniversary exhibition, taking place at Tokyo's Azabudai Hills Gallery until December 20.

Pre-orders opened in Japan on October 30 through Lawson and HMV&BOOKS, with reservations running until December 14, unless all 300 pieces sell out first.

But here's the catch. Despite being available to pre-order now, neither watch is expected to arrive until September 30 2027. That's almost eleven months of waiting for a watch that's already rather difficult to get hold of, particularly if you're shopping from outside Japan.

Still, for diehard Kojima fans, a limited-edition Seiko celebrating a decade of one of gaming's most recognizable studios might just be worth the wait.
